Chapter 9. The Silent Queens: Tones and Rings of the 600-cell and the 120-cell

First published in this book, from DICTO's studies of 4 November 2026 (studies/2026-11-04-harmonic-4d, 2026-11-04-hopf-interlock and 2026-11-04-pds-modes). Section 6 is retold from paper #109, Silent Running: Twisted Silence, Version 2, DICTO DOI 10.5281/zenodo.24272473 (all versions); its full statements, proofs and checks are in Appendix A.

Abstract. We test a whole four-dimensional solid with the harmonic fingerprint of #94, the 600-cell first. Seen from the centre, the corners of a four-dimensional solid are points on the 3-sphere, and each degree of harmonic on the 3-sphere is a tone. Main theorem. (A) The White Queen (the 600-cell, 120 corners) and the Red Queen (the 120-cell, 600 corners) are silent in every degree from 1 to 11 and sound first at degree 12: both are spherical 11-designs, and so is every orbit of their symmetry group. (B) The White Queen's corners are the 120 unit icosians, a group; its cyclic subgroups of orders 10, 6 and 4 split them into 12 great decagons, 20 great hexagons and 30 great squares, and the Hopf map sends these rings exactly onto the corners of the icosahedron, the dodecahedron and the icosidodecahedron. (C) The number of harmonics of degree k left unchanged by the White Queen's icosians equals, at every degree k from 0 to 120, the number of vibration modes of the Poincaré dodecahedral space given by Aurich, Lustig and Steiner; the White Queen's silence below degree 12 is the same fact as that space's missing low modes. All three are classical (Goethals and Seidel; Coxeter; Sadoc and Mosseri; Klein; Aurich, Lustig and Steiner); what is new here is the reading of the two Queens through them, checked from our own corners. Two further readings are numerical only and are labelled so: the tones of the White Queen's slices, and a station of 19-fold silence on the path between the Queens. (D) Under the symmetry of the 5-cell the White Queen's corners fall into five sets; two golden ones are silent in degrees that their symmetry does not force (5 and 7; 3 and 6), and the silence is forced by a twist of her group (#109, exact).

A solid can be listened to: its corners, seen from the centre, are notes on a sphere, and each degree of harmonic is a tone. In our space the icosahedron never sounds the fourth tone. One dimension up, the two Queens are quieter still: nothing sounds until the twelfth tone. The same silence shows when the White Queen is read as rings, and when she is read as the group of a possible shape of the universe.

1. The fingerprint in four dimensions

In #94 the corners of a solid in our space, seen from its centre, are weighted equally on the sphere and expanded in spherical harmonics; the strength Ql of each degree l is the solid's harmonic fingerprint, the bond-orientational order of Steinhardt, Nelson and Ronchetti. Take the test to a whole four-dimensional structure, the 600-cell first. On the 3-sphere the zonal harmonic of degree l is the Chebyshev polynomial of the second kind Ul, and for unit directions u1, …, uN we put

Ql2 = (1/N2) Σi,j Ul(ui·uj)/(l + 1),

the mean over all pairs. A single point gives Ql = 1. By the addition theorem Ql2 is a positive multiple of the sum, over an orthonormal basis of the degree-l harmonics, of the squared sums of each harmonic over the points; so Ql = 0 exactly when every harmonic of degree l sums to zero over the points. A set silent in every degree from 1 to t is a spherical t-design, a set of points that averages every polynomial of degree up to t exactly as the whole sphere does.

The test was calibrated on the regular four-dimensional solids (Table 1), whose design strengths are classical.

SolidCornersSilent degreesFirst tone
5-cell5up to 23
16-cell8up to 34
Tesseract16up to 34
24-cell24up to 5, and 106
White Queen, 600-cell1201 to 1112 (Q12 = 0.277)
Red Queen, 120-cell6001 to 1112 (Q12 = 0.087; Q20 = 0.106)

Table 1. The calibration: the degrees in which each regular four-dimensional solid is silent, as computed from its corners (study harmonic-4d). All agree with the known design strengths.

2. Main theorem, part (A): the two Queens are 11-designs

Theorem 1. The 120 corners of the White Queen and the 600 corners of the Red Queen are silent in every degree from 1 to 11 and sound at degree 12. More generally every orbit of their common symmetry group on the 3-sphere is silent in degrees 1 to 11.

Proof. The two Queens share the symmetry group H4 of order 14 400. For a harmonic f of degree l, the sum of f over an orbit equals the sum over the same orbit of the H4-average of f, which is an H4-invariant harmonic of degree l. The polynomials invariant under H4 are generated by invariants of degrees 2, 12, 20 and 30, and the one of degree 2 is the squared radius; so a harmonic invariant first occurs at degree 12. Below 12 the average is zero, every orbit sum vanishes, and by §1 every Ql with 1 ≤ l ≤ 11 is zero. At degree 12 the computed values Q12 = 0.277 (White Queen) and 0.087 (Red Queen) are not zero. ∎

The 600-cell's corners as an 11-design are classical (Goethals and Seidel 1981 [1]; Bannai's survey [2]); here they serve as the check that the test hears what is known. In our space the icosahedron is silent at degrees 2, 4, 8 and 14 among the even degrees (#94); the Queens are silent in every degree up to 11.

3. Main theorem, part (B): the White Queen in rings

Next come the tools of the harmonic study not yet used; the first is the Hopf map. The White Queen's 120 corners, with circumradius 1, are the unit icosians: as quaternions they form a group, the binary icosahedral group (checked on our corners).

Theorem 2. The left cosets of a cyclic subgroup of order 10, 6 or 4 split the White Queen's corners into great-circle rings: 12 decagons, 20 hexagons or 30 squares. The Hopf map q ↦ q u q−1, with u the axis of the subgroup's generator, sends each ring to one point of the 2-sphere, and the images are exactly the corners of the icosahedron (12 points, 5 nearest neighbours each), the dodecahedron (20 points, 3 each) and the icosidodecahedron (30 points, 4 each).

Proof. A cyclic subgroup ⟨g⟩ of order n lies on a great circle through 1, and each left coset q⟨g⟩ is its image under the rotation x ↦ qx, again a great circle; the 120 corners fall into 120/n cosets: 12, 20, 30. Right multiplication by a power of g commutes with u, which is the axis of g, so q gk u g−k q−1 = q u q−1, and a whole ring goes to one point. The images are the orbit of u under conjugation by the icosians, which acts as the icosahedral rotation group of order 60. An element of order 10, 6 or 4 is a rotation about a five-fold, three-fold or two-fold axis; the stabiliser of u has order 5, 3 or 2, so the orbit has 12, 20 or 30 points: the corners of an icosahedron, the face centres of an icosahedron (the corners of a dodecahedron), and its edge midpoints (the corners of an icosidodecahedron). ∎

The discrete Hopf fibrations of the 600-cell are classical (Coxeter [3]; Sadoc and Mosseri [4]); the counts, the one-point images and the nearest-neighbour numbers were checked on our corners (study hopf-interlock). Read as rings, the White Queen gives three solids of our space at once: the icosahedron, the dodecahedron and the icosidodecahedron, three stations of the golden cycle of DICTO's book The Golden Cycle (DICTO DOI 10.5281/zenodo.23752452).

4. Main theorem, part (C): the White Queen and the Poincaré space

Follow up an outside lead, the cosmic microwave background. The Poincaré dodecahedral space is the 3-sphere divided by the 120 icosians; it was proposed as the shape of space by Luminet and colleagues in 2003 [5] because it has no vibration modes of low degree, which would weaken the largest patterns in the microwave sky. Its modes of degree k are the harmonics of degree k on the 3-sphere left unchanged by the icosians.

Theorem 3. (a) For every degree k from 0 to 120, the number of harmonics of degree k on the 3-sphere invariant under left multiplication by the White Queen's 120 corners equals the multiplicity r(β), β = k + 1, of Aurich, Lustig and Steiner [6]: r(β) = β(⌊(β − 1)/10⌋ + ⌊(β − 1)/6⌋ + ⌊(β − 1)/4⌋ − ⌊(β − 3)/2⌋) for β in {1, 13, 21, 25, 31, 33, 37, 41, 43, 45, 49, 51, 53, 55, 57} or β odd and at least 61 (with r(1) = 1), and 0 otherwise. (b) The White Queen is silent in degrees 1 to 11 exactly because the Poincaré space has no mode of degree 1 to 11.

Proof. (a) The harmonics of degree k on the 3-sphere carry the representation Vk/2 ⊗ Vk/2 of left and right multiplication, so the dimension of the left-invariant part is (k + 1) times the mean, over the 120 icosians q = cos θ + u sin θ, of the character sin((k + 1)θ)/sin θ. Computed from our corners this number agrees with r(k + 1) at all 121 degrees k = 0, …, 120 (study pds-modes). Modes occur at k = 0, 12, 20, 24, 30, 32, 36, …: 13 of them at k = 12, 21 at k = 20, 122 at k = 60 and 363 at k = 120. (b) If f is a nonzero left-invariant harmonic of degree k and f(y) ≠ 0, then h(x) = f(xy) is again left-invariant of degree k (left and right multiplication commute), and the sum of h over the 120 icosians is 120 f(y) ≠ 0; so the icosians are not silent at k. Conversely, if there is no left-invariant harmonic of degree k, the average of any harmonic h over left multiplication is zero, and its value at 1, the mean of h over the icosians, vanishes. So the White Queen is silent at degree k exactly when the Poincaré space has no mode of degree k; by (a) the first mode is at k = 12. ∎

The invariant counts come from Klein's invariants of the binary icosahedral group, of degrees 12, 20 and 30 [7], and the mode counts of the Poincaré space are those of Aurich, Lustig and Steiner [6]; what this chapter adds is the cross-check from the White Queen's own corners and the reading of the White Queen as the Poincaré space's group. No claim is made about cosmology: Planck's 2015 search for the topology of space [8] tested flat topologies only, and the Poincaré space remains debated.

5. Numerical readings (not exact)

The two readings of this section are numerical. Their heights and positions are located on grids and are not exact; they are given as observations, with their precision, and are not part of the main theorem.

5.1 The tones of the White Queen's slices (numerical). Each slice of the White Queen, as in Chapter 6, is a solid of our space; its corners, seen from the slice's centre, have a three-dimensional fingerprint as in #94. With circumradius 1, on a grid of 1201 heights, with zeros located to about 0.002 (study harmonic-4d, slices600.py): corner first, every slice has icosahedral symmetry, so Q4 and Q8 are always silent, as they must be; Q6 falls silent as well at the heights |h| ≈ 0.128, 0.396 and 0.638, where the slices sound only from degree 10 up. Cell first, the slices have tetrahedral symmetry; Q4 is silent at |h| ≈ 0.157, 0.330, 0.583 and 0.737, and Q8 at |h| ≈ 0.137, 0.453, 0.829 and 0.854, never at the same height. So no cell-first slice hides icosahedral order in the way Icosaxes* does on the cube–octahedron sweep of #94, where Q4 and Q8 vanish together. The exact heights are open.

5.2 A station of deeper silence between the Queens (numerical). The path from the White Queen to the Red Queen of Chapter 7 can be written S(μ) = (600-cell) ∩ μ·(120-cell), with the 600-cell of circumradius 1 and the 120-cell its polar, for μ from r2 ≈ 0.8567 (r = 0.92562, the 600-cell's inradius), where S is the scaled Red Queen, to 1, where S is the White Queen. By Theorem 1 every stage is silent in degrees 1 to 11. For μ between (1 + φ/2)/2 = 0.904508 and 1 only the White Queen's corners are cut: S has 1440 corners, the points v + s(w − v) on her 1440 directed edges vw, with s = (1 − μ)/(1 − φ/2), the cut of Chapter 7. On this stretch Q12 falls to zero at μ* ≈ 0.924165 (Q12 below 9 × 10−5 there, on a scan of 400 points). Since H4 has no invariant harmonics in degrees 13 to 19 either, at μ* the 1440 corners would be silent in every degree from 1 to 19: a spherical 19-design, the deepest silence on the path. The cut there is deeper than the uniform cut 1/3 of the truncated 600-cell, so the station is not that solid. Q20 falls to zero near μ ≈ 0.9540, where Q12 is not zero. The 1440 points are the orbit of the reflection group W(H4) through a point on an edge of the 600-cell (stabiliser of order 10, orbit 14 400/10 = 1440), and such an orbit tuned to kill the degree-12 invariant is the classical route to a 19-design (Goethals and Seidel [1]; Bannai [2]). What is ours is only that this design is a station of the path between the Queens. The exact value of μ* is open.

6. Twisted silence: the golden sets

A boat running silent shows none of the lines a sonar listener is waiting for, while the rest of its sound goes on. The image is DICTO's, and two sets of the White Queen's corners do exactly this.

So far every silence came from symmetry: a set kept by a group G is silent in every degree in which G has no invariant harmonic. Here is a silence that symmetry does not give. Place the White Queen in the space of five coordinates summing to zero, where the symmetry group S5 of the 5-cell permutes the coordinates. S5 splits her 120 corners into five sets (Koca, Koç, Al-Barwani and Al-Farsi 2006): R, the 20 roots of A4; X and −X, the 20 permutations of (φ², φ², φ², φ, 0) and their negatives; F and −F, the 30 permutations of (φ², φ², 1, 1, 0) and their negatives (centre subtracted, one radius). In the notation of §1, a set is silent in degree k when Qk = 0; #109 writes the tone Tk, which is Qk2.

The golden silences (#109, Main theorem; exact; proof in Appendix A, §4). In every degree k ≥ 1: (i) X and −X are silent exactly in the degrees 1, 2, 5 and 7; (ii) F and −F exactly in 1, 2, 3 and 6; (iii) R exactly in 2 and every odd degree. The full symmetry group of X, and of F, is S5, of order 120, which forces only degrees 1 and 2.

The twist. Read the White Queen's corners as the group of 120 unit icosians (§3). The map σ that conjugates √5 to −√5 and then turns, σ(w, x, y, z) = (w̄, −x̄, z̄, ȳ), with the bar the conjugation, is an automorphism of the group that no rotation gives. Each of the five sets is a twisted class {a·x0·σ(a)−1}: it looks like an orbit of S5, but it is made from the icosians themselves by the twist. DICTO named the effect twisted silence. Its law (#109, Theorem 1, for any finite group and any automorphism of it; Appendix A, §3): a twisted class is silent in degree k exactly when the twisted character of every σ-stable piece of the degree-k representation vanishes on it; the pieces that σ exchanges never count. For the icosians the stable pieces have dimensions 1, 4, 5, 4 and 6, and degree 5 is the six-dimensional piece alone, degree 7 that piece with one that σ exchanges. The twisted character of the six-dimensional piece is zero on X, so X is silent in 5 and 7; that of the faithful four-dimensional piece, degree 3, is zero on F, and so is that of the other four-dimensional piece, which with an exchanged piece makes degree 6. One cubic identity and the orthogonality of the characters give both zeros at once: the two golden sets explain each other.

The same zeros can be read as Schur's spin characters of a double cover of S5, whose integrality alone forces one of the two golden pairs to be silent, and for X as Brauer's defect zero; #109 shows these are one mechanism seen three ways. DICTO's Double Vision, the Galois conjugation that sets a golden shape beside its twin, is what builds the twist; as a map of the set it forces only that every tone is rational. The silences are not part of a family. Turn the base point of the orbit like a dial, DICTO's sonar dial: on the two circles of base points with extra symmetry the double silences are isolated points, and the two golden signatures occur there only at X, F and their negatives (exact); a certified search off the circles found no others, though it is not exhaustive (Appendix A, §7).

The hulls of X and F are two golden solids, which DICTO named the Golden Truncated 5-cell (A) and the Golden Cantellated 5-cell (B) (Chapter 11, Figure 11.1). Where they lie in the White Queen is a separate question from why they are silent: Chapter 11 finds them hidden in her walls, and shows that being hidden comes from the kinds of their faces, not from their silence.

7. What is known and what is new

Known, credited. Spherical designs and the design strengths of Table 1, the 600-cell as an 11-design and the 19-designs from H4 orbits: Goethals and Seidel [1], Bannai [2]. The invariant degrees 2, 12, 20, 30 of H4, the icosians and the discrete Hopf fibrations of the 600-cell: Coxeter [3], Sadoc and Mosseri [4]. The invariants of the binary icosahedral group: Klein [7]. The Poincaré dodecahedral space as a model of space: Luminet et al. [5]; its mode multiplicities: Aurich, Lustig and Steiner [6]. The fingerprint Ql: Steinhardt, Nelson and Ronchetti, as cited in #94.

Shown here. Theorems 1–3 as readings of the two Queens, each checked from our own corners: the calibration of the four-dimensional fingerprint (Table 1), the three ring readings of the White Queen, and the agreement with Aurich, Lustig and Steiner at all 121 degrees up to 120. We did not find, in the sources checked, the harmonic fingerprint applied to the Queens, their slices or the path between them.

Section 6, credited. The split of the White Queen into five S5-orbits and the Galois form of the 5-cell's group on the icosians: Koca, Koç, Al-Barwani and Al-Farsi (2006), with the orbits' labels in Koca, Ozdes Koca and Al-Ajmi (2012); the algebraic core of the twisted-class law: Fel'shtyn and Troitsky, Lemma 5.1; the spin characters: Schur; defect zero: Brauer; designs and the zonal test: Delsarte, Goethals and Seidel. From #109 (in full in Appendix A): the golden silences and their proof by twisted characters, the three views as one mechanism, the dial. The image, the name twisted silence and the direction of the search are DICTO's.

Numerical only. The heights of §5.1 and the station μ* of §5.2.

8. Conclusion

Heard as tones, the two Queens are silent through degree 11, a silence that every orbit of their symmetry shares. Read as rings, the White Queen gives the icosahedron, the dodecahedron and the icosidodecahedron at once. Read as a group, she is the group of the Poincaré dodecahedral space, and her silence below degree 12 is that space's missing low modes. Between the Queens, a little past the truncated 600-cell, the silence numerically deepens to degree 19. Inside the White Queen, two golden sets are silent where their symmetry expects a sound, by twisted silence. Open: the exact heights of the silent slices, the exact value of μ*, and the fingerprint of the slices in the edge-first and face-first directions.

Sources checked

For the designs: E. Bannai's survey of spherical designs and J.-M. Goethals and J. J. Seidel, "Cubature formulae, polytopes, and spherical designs" (1981), checked for the credit of the 19-design (study harmonic-4d, 4 November 2026). For the Hopf rings: Coxeter; Sadoc and Mosseri (study hopf-interlock). For the Poincaré space: Planck 2015 results XVIII (arXiv 1502.01593), read; Luminet et al., Nature 425 (2003); Aurich, Lustig and Steiner, astro-ph/0412569, equations 26–27, used for the comparison; Roukema et al. (2008); Lew and Roukema (study pds-modes). DICTO's papers #62, #63, #65, #76 and #94.

* The truncated octahedron x = φ (Koca et al., 2015). DICTO's name: Icosaxes.

Chapter 10. The Queens' walls

Retold from paper #107, The Vacuus of the Queens, DICTO DOI 10.5281/zenodo.24126800, and paper #110, Walls of Silence: The Walls of the 4D 600-cell and 120-cell, Version 2, DICTO DOI 10.5281/zenodo.24272477 (all versions). The full statements, proofs and checks of #110 are in Appendix B.

The Vacuus in three kinds; the walls the two Queens share; the Mirror Law; Valley Charge

This chapter retells the two papers for the Queens. It keeps their status: exact means proved, or computed in exact arithmetic in ℚ(√5); floating-point checks are marked.

Join each face of a four-dimensional solid to its centre and it spans a flat slice through the centre. For most solids these slices are many and scattered. For the two Queens they are few and in order: one system of 360 walls through the centre carries every face of both.

1. The Vacuus, in three kinds

Let X be a polytope and O a chosen point inside it. The ridges of X are its faces of dimension two less than X: edges in three dimensions, two-dimensional faces in four. Each ridge spans with O one flat slice through O, its plate; the cone from O over the ridge is its fin. The Vacuus of (X, O), DICTO's name (#87, #105, #110), is the set of plates, each with the ridges on it. The centre must be stated: about the centre of the 600-cell (Schläfli, 1852) every one of its triangles lies on one of 60 plates, but measured from its own centroid the 600-cell less one corner has 915 plates, 900 of them carrying a single face.

A plate is a full sheet when with each ridge it carries the opposite ridge, a shared sheet when it carries two or more ridges but is not full, and a single fin when it carries one. DICTO named three kinds. (X, O) is of the heart kind when every plate is a full sheet, of the non-heart kind when every plate is a single fin, and of the inherited heart kind otherwise. In three dimensions each edge spans one plate through the centre. The octahedron's twelve edges lie four to a plate on three square sheets; the cube's twelve lie two to a plate, an edge and its opposite, on six diagonal planes; the tetrahedron's six lie one to a plate on six planes. The octahedron and the cube are of the heart kind, the tetrahedron of the non-heart kind (Figure 10.1).

Lemma 1 and Proposition 2 (the index law; #110, exact; proofs in Appendix B, §3). (X, O) is of the heart kind exactly when X is centrally symmetric about O. If G is the symmetry group of X fixing O, f a ridge with plate H, and Gf and GH the symmetries keeping f and H, then the ridges of the orbit G·f that lie on H number exactly |GH| / |Gf|.

The first holds because a ridge and its opposite span one plate, and a corner set closed under x ↦ −x has a centrally symmetric hull; the second is the orbit–stabiliser count read plate by plate, so the kind can be read off the symmetry of each plate. The two Queens are of the heart kind. The inherited heart kind has shared sheets without central symmetry: removing from the White Queen three corners at 120° to one another on one of her great hexagons leaves 1110 triangles on her 60 mirrors (3 mirrors with 14, 36 with 18 and 21 with 20), about her centre. In every shape on the Queens' corners tested so far that is not centrally symmetric, each shared sheet is a wall of the Queen; that this always holds is open (#110, Conjecture (i)).

2. The walls the Queens share

A wall of the White Queen is a flat slice through her centre of one of two kinds: one of her 60 mirrors, the slices across which she reflects into herself, or one of her 300 cross walls (DICTO's name), each parallel to a pair of her opposite cells and not a mirror. The normals of the mirrors point to her corners, those of the cross walls to her cell centres, the corners of the Red Queen. For the Red Queen the slices parallel to her cells are those same 60 mirrors, so the two Queens have one wall system of 360.

The cross walls are not mirrors, but they are no less the White Queen's own. They come from her cells as the mirrors come from her corners, and duality exchanges the two families. Each cross wall is also the middle slice of one of the 25 24-cells inscribed in her (Schoute; Denney et al. 2019), across a pair of its opposite octahedra, and the 25 × 12 = 300 such slices are exactly the cross walls (exact): the cross walls are the 24-cells' own middle walls. Their 300 normals, and the 60 of the mirrors, are the rays of Waegell and Aravind's Kochen–Specker sets (2011, 2014).

Which faces stand on the mirrors is answered by one rule, from #107.

The Queens' group is H4, whose Coxeter diagram is the chain 5–3–3. Wythoff's construction places a seed point with labels λi ≥ 0 on the four nodes and takes the hull of its images; the nodes with λi > 0 are the ringed nodes (Coxeter 1973; Champagne, Kjiri, Patera and Sharp 1995). The path of Chapter 7 is the seed sliding along the chain: the 120-cell (1, 0, 0, 0), the truncated 120-cell (1, 1, 0, 0), the rectified 120-cell (0, 1, 0, 0), the bitruncated 600-cell (0, 1, 1, 0), the rectified 600-cell (0, 0, 1, 0), the truncated 600-cell (0, 0, 1, 1) and the 600-cell (0, 0, 0, 1).

The Mirror Law (#107, Theorem 1; proved). Let P be the Wythoff polytope of a seed x, and J a node set of size n − 2 giving faces of P (so they have dimension n − 2: edges in three dimensions, 2-faces in four). The faces of type J lie on mirrors of the group if and only if the roots of the unringed nodes include one orthogonal to αj for every j in J.

The proof is short: the central hyperplane through a face of type J is a mirror exactly when some root is orthogonal to the seed and to every αj, and, since the coefficients of a root in the simple roots all have one sign, that happens only for a root of the unringed nodes (#107, Section 2). The law was checked against direct measurement for every seed of zeros and ones and every face type in A3, B3, H3, A4, B4, F4 and H4: 228 cases, no disagreement.

The Queens' faces (#107, Corollary 2; proved). Every face of a regular polytope lies on a mirror. The 1200 triangles of the 600-cell and the 720 pentagons of the 120-cell lie on the same 60 central slices, the 60 mirrors of H4: 20 triangles on each, and 12 pentagons on each.

Table 10.1. The faces on the mirrors along the path of Chapter 7, all seven solids (#107, Section 3; #110, Section 7). Each row follows from the Mirror Law. In each of the five stages between the Queens, the faces off the mirrors lie on 1200 plates that are on no wall at all.

At the midpoint, the bitruncated 600-cell, every mirror carries 20 triangles and 12 pentagons: the faces between its truncated tetrahedra, where the White Queen's cells were, and between its truncated icosahedra, where the Red Queen's were. Its hexagons, where a truncated tetrahedron meets a truncated icosahedron, lie on no mirror (#107, Corollary 3). The faces between like cells stand on the mirrors; the faces where the two kinds meet stand off them. Of the 15 uniform solids of H4 (Wythoff's construction; the list by Conway and Guy 1965, proved complete by Möller 2004) every one is of the heart kind, and only the two Queens have every plate on a wall (#110, Section 7; Appendix B).

3. Valley Charge

Between the two Queens, one solid has every wall and nothing else.

Cut each edge of the White Queen at 1/(1 + φ²) of its length from each end, a little less deeply than the uniform cut of 1/3. The solid that results stands on the path between the Queens, S(μ) = 600-cell ∩ μ·120-cell (DICTO's Hopscotch line; Chapter 9, §5.2), at μ = 1/2 + √5/5. DICTO named it Valley Charge.

Proposition 6 (Valley Charge; #110, exact; proof in Appendix B, §7). Valley Charge has 1440 corners, 720 cells, 2400 triangles and 1200 golden hexagons, and its Vacuus is exactly the 360 walls: 20 hexagons on each mirror and 8 triangles on each cross wall.

Its hexagons lie in the White Queen's triangles, so they stand on her mirrors; the cut is what puts its corner triangles on the cross walls, 8 on each. Cut at 1/3 or 1/5 instead, those triangles stand on no wall (floating point). Valley Charge is a different point of the path from the station μ* of deeper silence in Chapter 9: the walls and the tones pick out different stations. Valley Charge was not found in the sources checked.

4. What is known

Credited: the 600-cell and the 120-cell (Schläfli, 1852); reflection groups, Wythoff's construction and the faces of Wythoff polytopes (Humphreys 1990; Coxeter 1973; Champagne, Kjiri, Patera and Sharp 1995); the special cuts (Dutour Sikirić and Myrvold 2008); the 25 inscribed 24-cells (Schoute; Denney et al. 2019); the 60 and 300 directions (Waegell and Aravind 2011, 2014); the uniform solids of H4 (Conway and Guy 1965; Möller 2004). Not found in the sources read for #107 and #110: the Mirror Law as stated (its proof is short and may be known), the Vacuus reading of any of these shapes, the index law, the 24-cell reading of the cross walls and Valley Charge. The full lists of sources checked are in #107 and in Appendix B.

DICTO's: the Vacuus and its three kinds (heart, inherited heart, non-heart), the names the Queens, cross walls and Valley Charge, the ruling that cross walls count, the Hopscotch line, and the shadows as lattices of possible Vacuus (Chapter 5). Licence CC BY-SA 4.0.

The walls are now known. The next chapter asks what shapes are hidden in them.

Chapter 11. The Queens' cats

Retold from paper #110, Walls of Silence: The Walls of the 4D 600-cell and 120-cell, Version 2, DICTO DOI 10.5281/zenodo.24272477 (all versions), and paper #109, Silent Running: Twisted Silence, Version 2, DICTO DOI 10.5281/zenodo.24272473 (all versions). Their full statements, proofs and checks are in Appendices B and A.

The silent solids hidden in the White Queen's walls; hidden is not silent; weighing the cat; the cats in the Red Queen, and her own cats, their duals

The cat and the meat

A story, as usually told of Mulla Nasruddin. He brings home meat for supper, and when he comes back to eat it, it is gone. His wife says the cat ate it. He picks up the cat, puts it on the scales, and it weighs exactly what the meat weighed. "If this is the meat," he asks, "where is the cat? And if this is the cat, where is the meat?" The story is also told in Rumi's Masnavi, Book 5.

DICTO turned the story to the Queens, and this chapter uses it in one sense only. The meat is the Queen's structure: her walls, the flat slices through her centre on which her faces stand (Chapter 10). The cats are the shapes found hidden in those walls: the two golden solids A and B and the solid C3, each made from the White Queen's own corners (§1); their copies; and, in the Red Queen, their duals (§5). A cat is hidden in a wall system when every one of its faces lies on one of the walls. DICTO's idea of 16 November 2026 is that the silent solids of Chapter 9 had not been seen because they look like the White Queen's walls: the wood cannot be seen for the trees, and here the trees are in the walls.

Weighing the cat means asking whether the shapes and the walls are the same thing. The chapter gives three exact answers (§3). By corners, yes: the White Queen is exactly her five groups of corners, the corners of A, B, C3 and the mirror images of A and B. By walls, yes: A and B together use all her mirror walls. As solids, no: the five solids overlap and do not tile her.

1. The silent solids, hidden in the White Queen's walls

The five sets of Chapter 9 are the corners of five solids. With the edge of the 600-cell (Schläfli, 1852) √2 and its radius φ√2: the Golden Truncated 5-cell (A), DICTO's name, the hull of X, has 20 corners, 40 edges, 30 faces and 10 cells, 5 regular tetrahedra and 5 golden truncated tetrahedra whose hexagons have sides √2 and φ√2 in turn: the pattern of the truncated 5-cell with two edge lengths in the ratio φ. The Golden Cantellated 5-cell (B), DICTO's name, the hull of F, has 30 corners, 90 edges, 80 faces and 20 cells, 5 regular octahedra, 10 golden triangular prisms and 5 golden cuboctahedra: the pattern of the cantellated 5-cell, with the same two edge lengths. The hull of R is the uniform expanded 5-cell, labelled C3 here. With −A and −B these are the five S5-sets of the 600-cell's corners (Figures 11.1 and 11.2). The sets are Koca, Koç, Al-Barwani and Al-Farsi's (2006), who give the orbits by their Dynkin labels with Ozdes Koca and Al-Ajmi (2012), which fix the patterns and the golden edge ratio; the cells, edges and faces of the two hulls are worked out in #109, and the names and the reading of the hulls as Vacuus are DICTO's.

A shape on the White Queen's corners is hidden in her when every plate of its Vacuus, about her centre, is one of her walls; partly hidden when some are. Whether a shape is hidden is settled by the kinds of its faces:

Lemma 3 (the face lemmas; #110, exact; proof in Appendix B, §4). On the 600-cell's corners: (a) every triangle of three of its edges lies on a mirror, 20 on each, and so does every pentagon of the dual 120-cell, 12 on each (Chapter 10, #107 Corollary 2); (b) every golden rectangle with sides an edge and a radius lies on a mirror, 30 on each; (c) every triangle with sides equal to its radius lies on a cross wall, 8 on each; (d) a face fixed point by point by one of its reflections lies on that reflection's mirror.
Theorem A (the silent solids are hidden in the 600-cell; #110, exact; proof in Appendix B, §4). Every plate of A and of B is a wall of the 600-cell. A's 30 plates, 20 on its triangles and 10 on its hexagons, are mirrors. Of B's 80, the 10 on its edge triangles and the 30 on its golden rectangles are mirrors, and the 40 on its radius triangles are cross walls. The mirrors of A and of B share exactly the 10 mirrors of S5, and together they are all 60. C3's 70 faces lie on 5 cross walls, 14 on each. A and B are of the non-heart kind, C3 of the heart kind.

The proof is the face lemmas: A's triangles are edge triangles and each of its hexagons is fixed point by point by a swap of two coordinates, a reflection; B's faces are edge triangles, golden rectangles and radius triangles.

2. Hidden is not silent

A and B are hidden because their faces are faces of these kinds, not because they are silent. The same face lemmas hide shapes that are not silent at all, and the controls of #110 show how common being hidden is on the White Queen's corners (Appendix B, §§4, 7 and 8):

Known solids. The 24-cell, the snub 24-cell (Gosset, 1900) and the grand antiprism (Conway and Guy, 1965) are hidden in the White Queen. So is every special cut, the hull of what is left when corners no two of them adjacent are removed (Dutour Sikirić and Myrvold, 2008, who count 314,248,344 special cuts up to symmetry): every face of a special cut is a triangle of the White Queen, on a mirror, and the cut is of the heart kind when the removed corners come in opposite pairs, of the inherited heart kind otherwise (#110, Proposition 4). Every full-dimensional orbit hull of the five maximal subgroups of the Queens' group on her corners is hidden, except the 12- and 20-corner duopyramids (exact wall tests on floating-point hulls).

All five sets. All five S5-sets of Chapter 9 are hidden, R as well as X and F, while only X and F carry the twisted silences.

Chance. Of random sets of her corners, 100 samples of each size, the mean share of plates on no wall is 28% for 20 corners and 20% for 30 corners, and no sample was hidden; for 60 corners the share is 2%, and 60 of the 100 were hidden. So among sets the size of A and B, being hidden is rare (under about 3% at this sample size), but not impossible.

Off the corners. The ordinary S5-orbits of the same patterns, the permutations of (φ², φ², φ², 3/2, 0) and of (φ², φ², 6/5, 6/5, 0), have 20 of 30 and 70 of 80 plates on no wall; but they are not on the White Queen's corners, so they compare corners with non-corners, not twist with no twist. Of the other silent sets of #109, the four 30-point sets and the 60-point sets of the White Queen are hidden, and the two 240-point sets of the Red Queen are not (240 and 420 plates on no wall).

The walls do not hear the silence; they show where the silent shapes stand.

3. Weighing the cat

Do the cats, the hidden shapes, make up the White Queen whose walls hide them? There are three exact answers, one for each way of making.

By corners, yes. A, −A, C3, B and −B together are her 120 corners, 20 + 20 + 20 + 30 + 30, each once.

By walls, yes. A and B carry all 60 of her mirrors, and B carries 40 of her 300 cross walls (Theorem A). Their copies fill her faces exactly: the 5-cell's group sits in her in 60 ways, and in each it cuts one copy of each solid; each of her 1200 triangles is then a face of exactly one of the 120 copies of B and B's mirror image, and of exactly two of the 120 copies of A (Theorem B (d), §4).

By solids, no. The five hulls overlap. Of her volume, A takes (1 + 3φ)/40, B (3 + 5φ)/24 and C3 (14 + 7φ)/60; the five together cover 0.6504 of it, and what they share is a uniform bitruncated 5-cell, (23/750 + 23φ/1500) of it (exact). Her walls cut her into 4,291,200 = 14 400 × 298 chambers, each a pyramid from the centre over a piece of a cell (#110, Theorem C, exact, by Zaslavsky's count). Walls through the centre cannot cut out a solid round the centre, so A and B are never chambers; A's own 30 plates cut it into 3360 chambers, of which 120 are exactly the Coxeter chambers of her mirrors. The walls do not hand the shapes back: by corners and by walls the cats weigh what the meat weighs, and as solids they are not the meat.

4. The cats in the Red Queen

The Red Queen holds ten White Queens on her corners, in two groups of five (Chapter 8; the compound of ten 600-cells in the 120-cell is Coxeter's, Regular Polytopes, Table VII). Each group uses every corner once, so every corner is shared by two White Queens, one from each group. Each White Queen brings her own cats, so the Red Queen's cats come in two states, one for each group, and she holds both states at once. Every corner is in both states; cats of the two states share corners and share walls; but no cat is in both. DICTO's image for this is Schrödinger's cat, in two states at once: many half-alive cats living side by side. It is an image only, and no physics is claimed.

Theorem B (the cats in the Red Queen; #110, exact; proof in Appendix B, §5). (a) The 120-cell has exactly 10 inscribed 600-cells, every corner in exactly 2, in two states of 5 disjoint ones; two from different states meet in a 24-cell. (b) It holds exactly 1200 copies of A (with its mirror image), 1200 of B and 600 of C3, all inside the ten 600-cells, 120, 120 and 60 in each. (c) The walls of the ten 600-cells are 1560: the 120-cell's 60 mirrors, a cross wall of all ten; its 300 other walls, each a mirror of the 2 inscribed 600-cells through that corner axis; and 1200 slices that are not its walls, each a cross wall of exactly 2. Every copy of A is hidden in the 120-cell itself, its plates on the 300 only; every copy of B and of C3 is hidden in its own 600-cell but not in the 120-cell. (d) In each inscribed 600-cell the 120 copies of B split its 1200 triangles exactly.

Table 11.2. The cats in the Red Queen and the walls their plates use (#110, Table 3, exact). Each White Queen has 60 mirrors, from the Red Queen's 300 other walls, and 300 cross walls, 60 of them the Red Queen's mirrors and 240 from the 1200.

No copy lies in both states, since the White Queens of one state are disjoint, but copies of A and B from the two states share at most 6 corners and always share walls, two copies of A at least 2 of them, a copy of A and one of B at least 1, two copies of B at least 5 (two copies of C3 can share 12 corners and no wall). The two states meet in the 25 shared 24-cells, and there the cats come in halves. Fix the 5-cell's group in a White Queen of the first state: of her 5 shared 24-cells, one splits 0 A, 12 C3, 12 B and the other four 10 A, 2 C3, 12 B, so every shared 24-cell is half B in each of its two White Queens, whatever the choice. Over all 90,000 pairs of choices the corners of a shared 24-cell fall into 18 patterns, and in 1500 cases, 1 in 60, every corner is in the same set in both states (#110, Proposition 5). The cats of the two states are never one cat, and never apart.

5. Her own cats: the duals

The White Queens inside the Red Queen use her corners, but their edges are her diagonals, so their copies never make her edges or her pentagons. On 16 November DICTO proposed that a premise might be at fault: "Why the Red Queen but not the White? Is there perhaps a false underlying premise … something previously too elegant and perfect to question?" The premise was inclusion. The two Queens are related by duality, and DICTO's reading, which Theorem B′ confirms, is that the Red Queen's own cats are the duals of the White Queen's. Take the White Queen dual to her (corners along her cell centres) and the polar X* of each copy X about the common centre.

Theorem B′ (the dual cats; #110, exact in ℚ(√5) unless marked; proof in Appendix B, §5). (a) For each of the 600-cell's 60 copies of the 5-cell's group, the 120-cell is exactly A* ∩ (−A)* ∩ C3* ∩ B* ∩ (−B)*, and the five duals use its 120 cell walls once each. (b) A* is the 5-cell with a low pyramid on each cell (10 corners, 20 tetrahedral cells), its Kleetope (Grünbaum), 5 of its corners on the 120-cell; B* has 30 triangular bipyramids as cells; C3* has 20 cells of 8 corners. (c) Every plate of every dual copy lies on one of the 120-cell's own 60 mirrors, 10 per copy. A* and B* are of the inherited heart kind, C3* of the heart kind. (d) The fins of the 120 copies of A*, and those of the 120 copies of B*, each cover all 720 of its pentagons and all 1200 of its edges whole. The fewest copies that cover every pentagon are 16 of A*, 16 of B* or 22 of C3*: no cover by 15, 15 or 21 copies exists, certified by DRAT proofs from the SAT solver CaDiCaL checked by the independent proof checker drat-trim. No choice of copies covers each pentagon exactly once. (e) Dualising the copies inside the ten 600-cells instead covers no pentagon. (f) Each corner of the 120-cell is a corner of exactly one copy of A*: the 120 copies split its 600 corners into sets of 5, one corner from each of the ten inscribed 600-cells.

So the premise was the false part: the Red Queen's cats are not her inscribed White Queens but the duals of the White Queen's cats. Each Queen has her own cats, and duality relates them. What the golden form gives is the exact fit: the duals' cells on her cell walls, A*'s corners on her corners, and the Red Queen as the meet of the five duals. The cover of her pentagons comes from the pattern of the shapes, not from the golden proportions; orbits of the same pattern with other proportions cover the pentagons too, without the fit. DICTO proposed that the fins of the Vacuus form the Queens' skeletons, and they do, each Queen with her own cats: in the White Queen every triangle is a face of a copy of B (Theorem B (d)); in the Red Queen the fins of the dual copies cover every pentagon and every edge whole, on her own mirrors. One level down the two states return: the White Queen splits into 5 disjoint 24-cells in 10 ways, in two states of 5, with the same counts as the shared 24-cells above (Theorem B′ (f), Appendix B).

6. What is known

Credited: the 600-cell and the 120-cell (Schläfli, 1852); the 24-cell (Schläfli); the snub 24-cell (Gosset, 1900); the grand antiprism (Conway and Guy, 1965); the special cuts and their count (Dutour Sikirić and Myrvold, 2008); the five S5-orbits of the 600-cell (Koca, Koç, Al-Barwani and Al-Farsi 2006; Koca, Ozdes Koca and Al-Ajmi 2012), which give the Wythoff types of A and B but do not name or describe the hulls; the five maximal subgroups (Koca et al. 2006); the 25 inscribed 24-cells (Schoute; Denney et al. 2019); the compound of ten 600-cells in the 120-cell (Coxeter, Regular Polytopes, Table VII); the chamber count (Zaslavsky 1975); the Kleetope and the polar of a truncation (Grünbaum); the SAT solver CaDiCaL and the proof checker drat-trim. Not found in the sources read for #109 and #110: face lemmas (b) and (c) as statements about walls, the copies in the 120-cell and their three classes of wall, the triangle split, the half-cat patterns, the chamber counts of the 360 and 1560 walls and the dual cats (their walls, kinds, covers and the A* corner partition). The full lists of sources checked are in Appendices A and B.

DICTO's: the names Golden Truncated 5-cell and Golden Cantellated 5-cell, the idea that the silent shapes were hidden because they look like the White Queen's walls, the wood and the trees, the turning of the cat and the meat to the Queens and the image of the Red Queen's two states as Schrödinger's many half-alive cats, the reading of the shapes round the Queens as Vacuus, the survey of the 120-cell, the proposal that found the dual cats, the duals as the Red Queen's own cats, and the proposal that the fins form the Queens' skeletons. Plates

The plates give the shapes of Chapters 6 and 8 a page each. Plates 1–13 are the named slices of the White Queen, the 600-cell (Chapter 6); Plates 14–28 her fifteen Cleo shells (Figure 6.3); Plates 29–34 the named slices of the Red Queen, the 120-cell (Chapter 8); Plates 35–43 her nine layers of dodecahedra (Figure 8.1).

Each slice is drawn from its exact model in the DICTO solids dataset, version 6 (DICTO DOI 10.5281/zenodo.23790799), with its faces coloured by their number of sides as in the atlases of Figures 6.1 and 8.3: triangles ivory, four-sided faces blue, pentagons red, hexagons amber, seven-sided faces green, eight-sided faces violet, nine-sided faces teal, decagons pink. Heights are measured along each direction's own vector, as in the atlases, so they compare within one direction only. The names are DICTO's, after Lewis Carroll; the D-numbers are the permanent numbers of DICTO's catalogue. Plates 8, 11, 12 and 13 are sections in Alicia Boole Stott's series of 1900, under DICTO's names for them (Alicia, Humpty Dumpty, Unicorn, Bandersnatch); the first three carry no D-number, and Humpty Dumpty keeps the D85 it was given before this was known.

The shells and layers are seen in perspective, as in Figures 6.3 and 8.1, each drawn at its own scale, with the shells or layers inside the one shown in grey.

Epilogue. What has been shown

The way down and back

The book began with twelve dodecahedra round a thirteenth, and the gaps between them. DICTO pictured rays from the centre passing out through those gaps, gaps that should close in the fourth dimension, and the radial cell projection grew from that picture. The lead-up gave the pieces: three small pieces close the gaps between icosahedra (Chapter 1); of Aristotle's five pyramids only the cube's, and the tetrahedron's with the octahedron's, fill space (Chapter 2); face to face the five keep to their own solids, and every face-to-face tiling by them is a tiling by whole Platonic solids cut from their centres (Chapters 3 and 4).

The shadows of four dimensions then gave the shapes that gluing could not: variants of the convex deltahedra that join differently, and deltahedra rebuilt with every edge equal (Chapter 5). They also gave a key that the end of the book used again: every shadow keeps whole the walls that run along its view, flat planes through its centre, and across all the shadows those kept walls join into one lattice reaching all 360 walls of the Queens.

The Queens themselves were mapped completely. The White Queen has 62 slice patterns in her four main directions and four shadows, each resolving into an exact front half and back half, and her 600 cells stand in fifteen shells round one corner, the innermost twenty Cleo-Icos (Chapter 6); Alicia Boole Stott's series of 1900 is the first part of this atlas. Between the Queens lie five stages, built from the White Queen's own corners and mapped in 1402 slices (Chapter 7). The Red Queen is the White Queen's dual; she mirrors her by polarity, shadow for middle slice, holds the dodecahedron's pyramid at her core, and carries ten White Queens on her corners, in two states of five (Chapter 8). Aristotle's five pyramids are the cores of the four-dimensional regular solids, each with exactly its proportions. Heard as tones, both Queens are silent through degree 11, the White Queen as the group of the Poincaré dodecahedral space; and inside her two golden sets of corners are silent where their symmetry expects a sound, by twisted silence (Chapter 9).

The end returns to the centre where the book began. The walls of the Queens are flat slices through that centre, 60 mirrors and 300 cross walls, shared by the two of them; by the Mirror Law every face of both stands on the mirrors, and between them one solid, Valley Charge, has every wall and nothing else (Chapter 10). The silent solids of Chapter 9 stand in the White Queen's walls, every face on a wall, because their faces are of the kinds her walls carry. By corners they are the White Queen; by walls they carry all her mirrors and split her triangles exactly; as solids they overlap and are never her pieces. In the Red Queen the ten White Queens hold 1200 copies of each silent solid, no copy in both states and every copy sharing walls across them, and her own cats are their duals, whose fins cover every one of her pentagons (Chapter 11). The rays that once passed out through the gaps come back as walls, and the shapes found along the way were standing in them.

The results and their status

chapterresultstatus
1AXE, CLEO and FUJI close the gaps of thirteen icosahedra; FUJI is newexact
2which of Aristotle's five pyramids fill spaceproved
3face to face the pyramids keep to their solids; no new deltahedron from DICTO's pieces up to fourexact search
4every face-to-face tiling by the five pyramids is a tiling by whole Platonic solidsproved
5deltahedra from the shadows; each shadow keeps the walls along its view, one connected lattice of all 360exact
6the White Queen's atlas: 62 slice patterns, four shadows, fifteen Cleo shellsexact
7the five stages between the Queens and their atlas of 1402 slicesproved cuts; computed atlas
8Aristotle's pyramids as the cores of four dimensions; the Red Queen's atlas; ten inscribed White Queens in two statesexact
9the Queens as 11-designs, rings and the Poincaré space; twisted silence of the golden setsproved (classical readings checked); §5 numerical
10the three kinds and the index law; one system of 360 walls for both Queens; the Mirror Law; Valley Chargeproved or exact
11Theorem A (the silent solids hidden in the walls); hidden is not silent; weighing the cat (corners, walls, solids); Theorems B and B′ (the cats in the Red Queen and her dual cats); the chambersproved or exact; marked checks in floating point

Open problems

(1) Which tilings of space by the tetrahedron's and the octahedron's pyramids exist besides the octet cut up and its rearrangements (Chapter 2)? (2) Do gluings of five or more pieces with the regular solids, or seven or more of DICTO's pieces alone, give a new deltahedron, and do icosahedra with Antonius fill space (Chapters 3 and 4)? (3) Which of the new deltahedra of Chapter 5 tile space, alone or with others? (4) The atlases of Chapters 6, 7 and 8 cover the four main directions; the slices in other directions are not mapped, and the possible Vacuus of the shadows beyond the four main views are not listed. (5) The exact heights of the White Queen's silent slices, and the exact value of μ* where the path between the Queens is numerically a 19-design (Chapter 9, §5). (6) The inherited-heart rule: is every shared sheet of a shape on a Queen's corners that is not centrally symmetric a wall of the Queen (Chapter 10, §1)? (7) Are the 1200 walls of the ten White Queens that are not the Red Queen's own the cross walls of her 225 inscribed 24-cells? (8) The chambers: the classes and types of the 41,197 pieces that the 1560 walls cut in one chamber of the Red Queen's mirrors, as for the 298 of the White Queen's 360 walls (Chapter 11; Appendix B, §9)? (9) The open problems of twisted silence: the degree-8 sets, the E8 degree-4 unions, an exhaustive dial, and a complete list of the special silences of the icosians by prime-defect label (Appendix A, §10); and the further conjectures and questions of the walls (Appendix B, §9).

Appendix A. Silent Running: Twisted Silence

Paper #109 in full: its statements, proofs, tables and checks, from Version 2 (17 November 2026), DICTO DOI 10.5281/zenodo.24272473 (all versions; Version 2: 10.5281/zenodo.24295161). It is retold in Chapter 9, §6, and Chapter 11, and is given here with the paper named as its source. The theorem and section numbers are the paper's; its figures are numbered A.1 to A.6, and the two already in the book are referred to as Figures 9.1 and 11.1. Scripts, exact checks and data: https://github.com/DICTOR-Master/kaleidohedra/tree/9c9346c2/studies (research branch, commit 9c9346c2: folders 2026-11-15-fable-golden-a4, 2026-11-15-golden-a4-candidate, 2026-11-15-hidden-zeros-h4, 2026-11-15-twisted-silence-groups, 2026-11-15-quasicrystal-extinctions and 2026-11-15-law-giving-census; the paper's own checks, scripts/moment_check.py, and the second implementation, second-implementation/, are in its folder; the census re-derivation and the Proposition 6 check are in studies/2026-11-17-independent-checks).

Listening for hidden symmetries that silence specific harmonics of point sets in the 600-cell

The algebraic core of Theorem 1 is Fel'shtyn and Troitsky's Lemma 5.1 [5]; the spin characters are Schur's [2]; the defect-zero theorem is Brauer's [3]; the S5 split of the 600-cell, and the description of W(A4) through Galois-conjugate quaternions on which the golden twist rests, are Koca, Koç, Al-Barwani and Al-Farsi's [32] (also Koca, Ozdes Koca and Al-Ajmi [4]); they give those orbits as W(A4) polytopes by their Dynkin labels ([4, §4.1, eq. (30)]), which fix the combinatorial types and the golden edge ratio; the cells, edge lengths and face counts of the two solids, the Golden Truncated 5-cell (A) and the Golden Cantellated 5-cell (B), are worked out here, and their reading as Vacuus is DICTO's; the central-character formula behind Theorem 1 is Frobenius's; the invariant-harmonic test is Sobolev's [33]; designs and their zonal test are Delsarte, Goethals and Seidel's [6]; designs of harmonic index are Bannai, Okuda and Tagami's [8], and the silence signature is Misawa and Nishimura's harmonic strength [39].
DICTO: the title, the opening image, the name twisted silence, the names Golden Truncated 5-cell and Golden Cantellated 5-cell, the proposals, and the method and direction of the search: echo location, listening for the silences and turning the sonar dial in two and three directions, which locates the network in the 600-cell.

Abstract. A finite set of points on a sphere is silent in degree k when every harmonic polynomial of degree k sums to zero over it. Symmetry forces some silences: a set with the symmetry of a group G is silent in every degree in which G has no invariant harmonic. We show that a second, hidden kind of symmetry forces more. Read the 120 corners of the 600-cell (Schläfli, 1852) as the binary icosahedral group 2I of unit quaternions. The symmetry group S5 of the 5-cell splits them into five orbits of 20, 20, 20, 30 and 30 corners (Koca et al. [32, 4]). Each orbit is a twisted conjugacy class {a·x·σ(a)−1}, where σ is the outer automorphism of 2I: the Galois conjugation √5 ↦ −√5 followed by a rotation. Main theorem: the 20 permutations of (φ², φ², φ², φ, 0) are silent in exactly the degrees 1, 2, 5 and 7, and the 30 permutations of (φ², φ², 1, 1, 0) in exactly 1, 2, 3 and 6, in all degrees. Their symmetry is S5 and nothing more, and S5 forces only degrees 1 and 2: ordinary symmetry does not explain degrees 5 and 7, or 3 and 6. Those silences are forced by twisted characters (Theorem 1, a silence criterion for twisted classes of a finite group, which is Frobenius's central-character formula applied to the group extended by the twist). Three accounts of the mechanism (twisted characters of 2I, Schur's spin characters of a double cover of S5 with an integrality argument, and Brauer's defect zero) are complementary explanations of one piece of representation theory, not three independent proofs (Proposition 3). Galois conjugation, which DICTO calls Double Vision, builds the twist; as a map of the set it forces only that every tone is rational. A census of 3232 point sets in five H4 and E8 polytopes, exhaustive over its listed subgroup-orbit and union families rather than over all point subsets, leaves three open families. A classification shows where genuine twists exist (I2(n) for n = 5, 7, 9, 10, 11 and n ≥ 13, H3 and H4 through Galois conjugation, and Sym6 through its exceptional automorphism, which adds no silence). On the two edge circles of a dial the double silences are isolated points (exact), and a certified search found no others. The forced silences are a group-theoretic analogue of the systematic absences of a nonsymmorphic space group; this is an analogy, not a diffraction result, and a quasicrystal decorated with the silent sets shows no extinct Bragg peaks.

1. Running silent

A U-boat sits on the sea bed and waits out the hunt. Overhead the enemy's ships listen with sonar and drop depth charges wherever they hear it. No boat can be silent at every frequency. The sonar operator listens past the hiss of the sea for tonals: sharp lines at the particular frequencies of a boat's pumps, motors and shafts, which stand out and carry far [31]. A boat running silent shows none of the lines the operator is listening for, while the rest of its sound goes on, and to the listener above it passes as one more shape on the sea bed. The image is DICTO's, and it describes the point sets of this appendix.

A set of points on a sphere has a tone in every degree k, the size of its degree-k harmonic content. A generic orbit of a symmetry group rings in every degree the group allows. The golden sets of this appendix have no more symmetry than their neighbours, so a listener who knows their symmetry expects them to ring in degrees 5 and 7, or 3 and 6. They are silent there (Figure 9.1). The degrees are the frequencies, the tones are the lines, and the listener is anyone who knows the set's symmetry. As with the boat, no finite set is silent in every degree, and the rest of its tones go on. Unlike the boat, the sets do not choose their silence: the twist forces it. Their disguise is a twist: each is a twisted conjugacy class of the binary icosahedral group, a set that looks like an orbit of the 5-cell's symmetry group but is built from the group itself by a map that turns √5 into −√5. That map is DICTO's Double Vision [1], the move that places a golden shape and its Galois twin in one space. Here it does not silence anything on its own; it builds the twist, and the twist silences.

Two kinds of symmetry are at work. The ordinary symmetry of X and F is the group S5 of the 5-cell, which permutes the five coordinates. It forces their silence in degrees 1 and 2, because S5 has no invariant harmonic there; but it has an invariant harmonic in every degree from 3 on, so it cannot explain degrees 5 and 7 of X, or 3 and 6 of F. Those come from a twisted symmetry: the 600-cell's own group 2I acting on itself by x ↦ a·x·σ(a)−1, where σ is not an inner automorphism. This appendix is about that second kind.

2. Tones, silence and labels

Tones. For a finite set S on the unit sphere of ℝn and a degree k ≥ 1, the tone is Tk(S) = |S|−2 Σx,y∈S Gk(⟨x, y⟩)/Gk(1), with Gk the Gegenbauer polynomial of the sphere (the Chebyshev polynomial of the second kind Uk on S³ ⊂ ℝ⁴). Tk(S) ≥ 0, and Tk(S) = 0 exactly when every harmonic polynomial of degree k sums to zero over S (Delsarte, Goethals and Seidel [6]). We then say S is silent in degree k, or kills k. The silence signature of S is the set of k it kills. A t-design kills 1, …, t; a set killing a chosen list T of degrees is a spherical T-design (Miezaki [38]), or a design of harmonic index T in the sense of Bannai, Okuda and Tagami [8] and Zhu et al. [9], who note that Delsarte and Seidel's designs admitting indices [37] are essentially the same notion. The silence signature is what Misawa and Nishimura call the harmonic strength Hst(S) [39].

Forced silence. If G is a finite group of isometries fixing S, then S kills every degree in which G has no invariant harmonic (counted by Molien's series [26]; for orbits this is the method of Goethals and Seidel [7]). An orbit G·v kills degree k exactly when every G-invariant harmonic of degree k vanishes at v (Sobolev's theorem [33], stated for any finite group as in Nozaki and Sawa [35, Theorem 2.1]; [7]). A silence is unforced when the full symmetry group of S has an invariant harmonic in that degree.

The 600-cell as a group. The 120 unit icosians, the unit quaternions ½(±2, 0, 0, 0), ½(±1, ±1, ±1, ±1) and ½(0, ±1, ±φ−1, ±φ) with even permutations, form the binary icosahedral group 2I and are the corners of the 600-cell [14, 15, 16]. φ = (1 + √5)/2. On S³ = SU(2), harmonic degree k corresponds to the representation Vk = Symk(ℂ²) of dimension k + 1: the degree-k harmonics are the matrix coefficients of Vk, and Uk(Re g) is its character (Peter–Weyl). 2I has nine irreducible representations, of dimensions 1, 2, 2′, 3, 3′, 4, 4s, 5 and 6 (4s and 6 faithful); their pattern inside Vk is McKay's [13].

Twisted classes. Let G be a group and σ an automorphism of G. The twisted class of x0 ∈ G is O = {a x0 σ(a)−1 : a ∈ G}. For σ the identity it is an ordinary conjugacy class. An irreducible representation π of G is σ-stable when π∘σ ≅ π; then some Jπ, unique up to a scalar, satisfies π(g) Jπ = Jπ π(σ(g)), and the twisted character is χ̃π(x) = tr(π(x) Jπ−1). When σ is an involution we scale Jπ so that Jπ² = 1; a zero of χ̃π does not depend on the scaling.

Labels. Exact: proved, or computed in exact rational or Q(√5) arithmetic. Certified: located numerically and certified by a Krawczyk interval test [24]. Numerical: floating point. Quoted: a known result used from the literature and credited; where it was not re-read for this appendix, the reference says so.

Reproduction. Every exact claim has a script and a log in the folders of the meta line. The central computations (the twisted classes, Tables A.1 and A.2, the Main theorem to degree 60, Theorem 1's criterion on every class of 2T, 2O and 2I, Propositions 3, 5 and 7, the Sym6 case of Proposition 6 and the edge circles of §7) were redone by a second implementation written separately from the study scripts (second-implementation/, exact arithmetic, its own report). The census was re-derived by a third program, also written separately (studies/2026-11-17-independent-checks/109-census): it recomputed the zero lists of all 808, 144, 257 and 541 sets of the 600-cell, 24-cell, snub 24-cell and E8 universes, and they agree; the 1482 sets of the 120-cell agree by size, zeros and stabiliser order (set by set was not possible from the stored data); its own orbit catalogue agrees with the census in both directions; and it confirms every zero of Table A.3. The count 3232 belongs to the census's union rule (§6) and was not reproduced independently. The census is therefore partly reproduced. All three programs were written with an AI assistant; the proofs have not had specialist review, so the results are reproducible, not independently refereed.

3. Main results

Main theorem (the golden silences). Exact. Under the coordinate permutations S5, the 600-cell splits into five orbits (Figure A.1): R, the 20 roots of A4 (antipodal); X and −X, the 20 permutations of (φ², φ², φ², φ, 0) and their negatives; F and −F, the 30 permutations of (φ², φ², 1, 1, 0) and their negatives (coordinates in ℝ⁵ with the centre subtracted, one radius). In every degree k ≥ 1:
(i) X and −X are silent exactly in the degrees 1, 2, 5 and 7;
(ii) F and −F are silent exactly in the degrees 1, 2, 3 and 6;
(iii) R is silent exactly in degree 2 and every odd degree.
The full symmetry group of X, and of F, is S5 of order 120. S5 forces only degrees 1 and 2. Degrees 5 and 7 of X, and 3 and 6 of F, are forced by the twisted characters of 2I (Theorem 2), not by any isometry.

Theorem 1 (twisted silence). Exact, general. Let G be a finite group, σ an automorphism of G, x0 ∈ G and O its twisted class. Let Π be a finite-dimensional complex representation of G (for instance the restriction to G of a representation of a compact group H ⊃ G). Then Σx∈O Π(x) = 0 if and only if χ̃π(x0) = 0 for every σ-stable irreducible constituent π of Π. Constituents that are not σ-stable never contribute. A union of twisted classes O1, …, Om has Σ Π(x) = 0 if and only if Σi |Oi| χ̃π(Oi) = 0 for every σ-stable π. On S³ = SU(2) with Π = Vk, this is silence in degree k.

Theorem 2 (the 600-cell). Exact. The S5-orbits of the 600-cell are the five twisted classes of 2I under its outer automorphism σ(w, x, y, z) = (w̄, −x̄, z̄, ȳ), where the bar is the Galois conjugation √5 ↦ −√5. The σ-stable irreducibles are 1, 4, 5, 4s and 6; the pairs (2, 2′) and (3, 3′) are exchanged by σ. Their twisted characters on the five classes are those of Table A.1, and the Main theorem follows from Theorem 1 and the restriction of Vk to 2I (Table A.2).

Proposition 3 (one mechanism, three views). Exact for (a), (b) and the integrality step; Schur's rule and Brauer's theorem quoted. The three views below are complementary explanations of one mechanism, not three independent proofs of the Main theorem. Let G+ = 2I ⋊ ⟨σ⟩, a group of order 240 with centre {±1} and G+/{±1} ≅ S5, a double cover of S5.
(a) The twisted class of x0 is the G+-class of x0σ in the outer coset, and χ̃π(x0) is, up to a fixed sign, the character of the extension of π to G+ at x0σ. So the twisted character table of 2I is the table of G+ on its odd coset, and the faithful rows 4s and 6 are the spin characters ⟨3, 2⟩ and ⟨4, 1⟩ of the double cover.
(b) Integrality alone forces one zero in each spin row: with u = |χ̃6(X)|² and v = |χ̃6(F)|², 2u + 3v = 6 in whole numbers, so (u, v) = (0, 2) or (3, 0). Exactly one of the two golden pairs is therefore silent in degrees 5 and 7.
(c) Which pair is fixed either by one cubic identity, p3(F) = 0, or by Schur's odd-class rule [2]: a spin character ⟨λ⟩ is nonzero on odd elements only on cycle type λ.
(d) For X there is a third reading: 6 is of 3-defect zero in G+ (240 = 2⁴·3·5 and 3 | 6), so by Brauer's theorem [3] its character vanishes on every element of order divisible by 3; X lies over the S5 class of cycle type (3, 2), of order 6. F's zero (4s on 4-cycles) has no such reading and rests on (b), (c).
Double Vision enters only through σ: it is the Galois part of the twist that builds G+ from 2I. As a map of the 600-cell (Φ = φ²·Galois) it fixes all five orbits and forces only that every tone is rational.

How the three views divide the work. The proof of the Main theorem needs only Theorem 1, Table A.2 and the zeros of Table A.1, which are computed exactly (Theorem 2). Proposition 3 explains those zeros. View 1, the twisted characters of 2I, is that computation and covers every zero. View 2, Schur's spin characters of the double cover G+, shows by integrality alone that one of the two golden pairs must be silent in the spin degrees (b); the cubic identity p3(F) = 0, or Schur's odd-class rule, decides which (c). View 3, Brauer's defect zero, explains only X's zero in degrees 5 and 7 (d), and says nothing about F. All three describe one group of order 240.

Theorem 4 (degree linkage on the 600-cell). Exact (proved below; the rational kernels were also computed for k ≤ 20). For a real weighting w of the 600-cell put Tk(w) = Σx,y wxwy Uk(⟨x, y⟩)/(k + 1), which vanishes exactly when the degree-k harmonic part of w does (for a subset, w its indicator, this is |S|² Tk(S)). For every real weighting, T7 = 0 implies T5 = 0, and T9 = 0 holds exactly when T15 = 0; for every rational weighting (in particular every subset), T7 = 0 also implies T1 = 0. So the golden double silence is one rational condition, not two. The first implication has a reason in Table A.2: V7 = 2′ + 6 and V5 = 6, and Galois conjugation exchanges the 2′-part and the 2-part of a rational set, so a rational set with no 2′-part has no 2-part (T1 = 0).

Proposition 5 (the Double-Vision class pair). Exact, with the 2I character values quoted. Let C be a conjugacy class of 2I of elements of order 5 or 10 (12 points) and σC its Galois image. The 24-point set C ∪ (−σC) is silent exactly in degrees 3, 4, 5, 9 and 15, in every degree k ≥ 1. Odd degrees see only the faithful constituents, whose characters on −σC are −σ(χ(C)), so the block sums are χ(C) − σχ(C): they vanish exactly when every constituent of Vk is rational on C (4s and 6 are; 2, 2′, 3, 3′ are not). In even degrees only the 5, with character 0 on C, vanishes. By contrast C ∪ −C kills every odd degree and 4, and C ∪ σC kills only 4.

Proposition 6 (where a genuine twist exists). Exact for (a), (b), the dihedral reduction, the A5 case and the irreducibility counts; the list of automorphisms of finite Coxeter groups is quoted [17, 18]. Let ρ be the defining representation of a finite group G ⊂ O(n). (a) If ρ∘σ ≅ ρ, every twisted class is an ordinary conjugation orbit moved by an isometry. (b) If ρ∘σ ≅ ρ ⊗ λ for a one-dimensional character λ ≠ 1, every twisted class lies in two parallel hyperplanes (a latitude pair). (c) Otherwise, for the reflection representation of a finite irreducible Coxeter group and for the finite subgroups of SU(2), the twist either comes from Galois conjugation, which exists only for I2(n) (n = 5, 7, 9, 10, 11 or n ≥ 13), H3 and H4, or is the exceptional outer automorphism ξ of W(A5) = Sym6, whose five twisted classes on S²⁴ kill only degree 1 for k ≤ 40, as the twist group forces. On S³, 2T and 2O fall under (a) and (b) (Σχ²/|G| = 2); binary dihedral classes lie on great circles; and 2I is the only case whose twisted action on its own sphere is irreducible (Σχ²/|G| = 1).

Proposition 7 (moment rigidity). Exact. If a set S on the unit sphere S³ is silent in degrees 1, 5 and 7, then for every unit vector u the odd moments pk(u) = Σx∈S ⟨x, u⟩k satisfy p5(u) = p3(u) and p7(u) = (7/8)·p3(u). In particular this holds for X scaled to unit radius.

In words: pk(u) measures how the set leans towards the direction u, weighted more and more towards the points nearest u as k grows. Silence in degrees 1, 5 and 7 removes every odd part of the set's shape except the degree-3 part, so along every direction the odd moments of orders 3, 5 and 7 are fixed multiples of one function: the set's odd lean has the same pattern at every order, the pattern of a single cubic.

4. Proofs

Proof of Theorem 1. Put S = Σx∈O Π(x). Every point of O is reached |Stab| times as a runs over G, so |Stab|·S = Σa∈G Π(a) Π(x0) Π(σ(a))−1. All three factors preserve the G-isotypic blocks of Π (x0 and σ(a) lie in G), and on a block π ⊗ ℂm they act as π(·) ⊗ 1, so it is enough to treat one copy of π. On a block of type π the sum Sπ = Σa π(a) π(x0) π(σ(a))−1 satisfies π(g) Sπ = Sπ π(σ(g)), so it intertwines π∘σ with π. By Schur's lemma Sπ = 0 unless π is σ-stable, and Sπ = c·Jπ if it is. Taking the trace of Sπ Jπ−1 and using π(σ(a))−1 Jπ−1 = Jπ−1 π(a)−1 gives c = |G| χ̃π(x0)/dim π. So the block of S is |O| χ̃π(x0) Jπ/dim π, which vanishes exactly when χ̃π(x0) = 0 (Jπ is invertible), and the union statement follows by adding blocks. On S³ the degree-k harmonics are exactly the functions x ↦ tr(M Vk(x)) (both spaces have dimension (k + 1)²), so Σx∈O tr(M Vk(x)) = tr(M S) vanishes for every M exactly when S = 0: S = 0 is silence in degree k. ∎ A step-by-step audit of this proof, with its assumptions (complex coefficients, Jπ unique up to a scalar by Schur's lemma because π is irreducible, finiteness of G for the sum), is in the paper's folder, second-implementation/THEOREM1-AUDIT.md; it found no gap.

The step that twisted class functions are spanned by twisted characters is Fel'shtyn and Troitsky's Lemma 5.1 [5], with their Theorem 4.3 (the number of twisted classes equals the number of σ-stable irreducibles; for finite groups they refer to Fel'shtyn and Hill [36]). Theorem 1 is classical in substance: by Proposition 3 (a), Σx∈O Π(x) is the image of a class sum of G ⋊ ⟨σ⟩ in its coset Gσ, and a class sum acts on an irreducible representation by the scalar |C|χ(g)/χ(1) (Frobenius's central character); characters on the coset Gσ are the subject of Shintani descent. For σ the identity it is the statement that a conjugacy class kills Π exactly when every constituent has character zero on it. What is new here is its reading as silence of harmonic sums and its use for design zeros that symmetry does not force.

Proof of Theorem 2. The rotations of the 600-cell are x ↦ a x b−1 with a, b ∈ 2I [16]. The A5 inside S5 acts irreducibly on the 4-space, so its preimage in 2I × 2I is a copy of 2I that projects isomorphically onto both factors (2I has no subgroup isomorphic to A5), that is {(a, σ(a))} for an automorphism σ of 2I, and σ is not inner (an inner σ would fix an axis). This Galois form of W(A4) is in Koca, Koç, Al-Barwani and Al-Farsi [32, eq. (31)]: quaternion pairs [p, ∓a p′ a] built from p ∈ 2I and its Galois conjugate p′ (√5 ↦ −√5), with a = (e2 − e3)/√2, together with the split of the 600-cell into 20, 20, 20, 30 and 30. They do not treat the orbits as twisted conjugacy classes or consider harmonic sums. Checked exactly: the map σ(w, x, y, z) = (w̄, −x̄, z̄, ȳ) is an automorphism of 2I (it is an involution), its twisted classes have sizes 20, 20, 20, 30, 30, and X and F are congruent to two of them (exact Gram isomorphism). The 4-space is 2 ⊗ 2′, the product of the two Galois-conjugate 2-dimensional representations, so σ exchanges 2 with 2′ and 3 with 3′; these four never contribute (Theorem 1). There are five twisted classes and five σ-stable irreducibles (1, 4, 5, 4s, 6), as Fel'shtyn–Troitsky's count requires. Table A.2 is computed exactly. Table A.1 is computed exactly from the class sums: with Jπ² = 1, χ̃π(O)² = dim π · Σx,y∈O χπ(xy)/|O|². The nonzero spin values are imaginary, ±i√3 and ±i√2: x0σ has order 6 on X, so a character value there lies in ℚ(√−3), which does not contain √3. Their moduli, and the zeros, also follow as below without quoting any table.

Zero pattern from one identity. Faithful twisted characters change sign under x ↦ −x, so they vanish on the antipodal class R and take values (a, −a, b, −b) on (X, −X, F, −F) for 4s and (c, −c, d, −d) for 6. Orthogonality over the 120 elements of the outer coset gives 40|a|² + 60|b|² = 120, 40|c|² + 60|d|² = 120 and 40ac̄ + 60bd̄ = 0. The S5-invariant cubic harmonic is the centred power sum p3, and p3 vanishes at (φ², φ², 1, 1, 0) (exact), so F kills degree 3 (Sobolev's criterion). Since V3 = 4s, b = 0; then |a|² = 3, so c = 0 and |d|² = 2. Hence 6 vanishes on X: X kills 5 (V5 = 6) and 7 (V7 = 2′ + 6). The two golden sets explain each other.

Complete lists. X kills odd k exactly when 4s is absent from Vk, which happens for k = 1, 5, 7, and even k exactly when none of 1, 4, 5 occurs, which happens for k = 2 only. F kills odd k exactly when 6 is absent (k = 1, 3), and even k exactly when neither 1 nor 5 occurs (k = 2, 6); the 4 vanishes on 4-cycles because the standard character of S5 is (fixed points − 1) = 0 there. Why a finite table settles every degree: there is a polynomial of degree 12, Klein's invariant form [14], that every element of 2I leaves fixed. Multiplying by it embeds Vk in Vk+12 as 2I-representations, so a constituent present in degree k is present again in degree k + 12, and in every later degree of the same residue mod 12. X rings in an odd degree as soon as 4s is present, and 4s is present at k = 3, 9, 11, 13, 17 and 19, one degree in each odd residue class mod 12; so every odd k ≥ 13 rings, and the odd silences are 1, 5 and 7. The same count for the even degrees (1, 4 or 5 present from k = 4 to 14) and for F (6 present at k = 5, 7, 9, 11, 13, 15; 1 or 5 present at k = 4, 8, 10, 12, 14, 18) leaves only the listed silences, and the table to k = 30 contains all of these witnesses. The predicted lists equal the direct exact sums for all five classes and every k ≤ 30. The full symmetry group of X is the automorphism group of its exact Gram matrix, of order 120, and the harmonic Molien series of S5 on the 4-space, 1/((1−t³)(1−t⁴)(1−t⁵)), has no invariant in degrees 1 and 2 and at least one in every degree k ≥ 3. ∎
